ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u13	Thank you. Please respond to the Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u14	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen! High Izbo! Thank you very much for this question, because it is actually an opportunity to explain or correct certain things. The way in which the Member describes what is planned may indeed raise some doubts or concerns, which is why I am pleased to be able to explain this and possibly address these concerns. First of all, the first information is that the System of State Registers, as it is now defined, includes three databases, i.e. the PESEL database, the Register of Personal Evidence and the Offices of Civil State Base. On the other hand, in the Integrated Information Technology Programme last year, the government adopted a directional strategy to extend the PESEL registry system to include further registers, with the overarching aim of building a single central location, a reference point to which other information systems could refer, in order to avoid duplication of the same information in many places. So that our actions generally, in principle, are part of the plan to expand this data set. And now, with regard to the first question, the question about collecting data about the actual composition of households, first of all I would like to explain that it is not planned to collect information just as it was described. It's about the actual composition of households. The only thing we're planning on doing at this time is gathering information or building connections between PESEL children and their biological parents. This may seem strange, but unfortunately, in the current system, both BUSC and PESEL, such information is not stored directly and simply does not allow certain automated functions and checks that would allow in already diagnosed situations and services, such as the service related to the provision of 500+, to substitute certain things to the citizen who completes the application, automatically to verify and supplement it. At this point in time, we are not able to build such a service because we do not store this data. And here's the primary target. It is simply to write in the register of this information so that it can be referred to in a simple way and through a service, which is then also called on the Internet, e.g. on the form. As I said, for the time being, we are talking about writing down links about biological relationships, and we are considering supplementing this information with data on court judgments related to the granting or withdrawal of parental rights. This, on the other hand, is a more complicated operation, because it requires integration into the case or court record, which at this point in time, at this stage of work, is not possible. However, the work of the Ministry of Justice and Home Affairs is also ongoing. As far as the register of contact data is concerned, we are talking here about a new register and data collection only with the consent of the citizen. So we're not talking about the fact that somewhere in the background without his knowledge these data will be downloaded from other places and will be used without his knowledge. So we're talking about giving the citizen a chance to pass on information, their contact details, so that other information systems can use this data and use it to communicate with the customer in a way other than the traditional paper route. So we're talking, for example. to send notifications or reminders or other information if the citizen has previously agreed to receive such information. This does not exclude and at the same time does not stop other works that are ongoing on the so-called. An e-box or an e-service. However, this is the first step to make sure that there is just one central place to which another system can appeal so that other systems do not have to build their own registers and, from the citizen's perspective, so that the citizen, giving the data in one place, can know that these data will be used in other systems, e.g. without the need to update this data repeatedly by the citizen. With regard to securing the systems of state registers, especially the new ones, I can assure you that all work on the new registers will have to meet the same requirements that meet the current state registers. This applies to appropriate procedures at each stage of the software generation cycle. So we're talking about adequate safeguards, ensuring certain safety tests or security audits in the process of preparing a solution, including in operation. Here the details are precisely for security reasons I cannot talk about, but I assure you that these works, because of the fact that they concern registers, are directly statutory, properly secured. As regards the level of security required, they shall also be subject to the control of the Internal Security Agency. And the fourth question about blockchain technology. In fact, we see a great deal of potential and opportunities to start using this technology in our solutions. There is a special stream called Blockchain and Kryptovaluty [[♪ The bell ♪]] under the "Paper-to-Digital Polish" programme, and in the framework of this work are carried out jointly with experts on how this technology can be used in the country, including in the System of State Registers. However, at this point in time, I cannot clearly determine when exactly this is going to happen. On the other hand, this will certainly be seriously considered. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u21	Thank you. Please respond, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u22	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I will start by saying that the questions that Mr Sachaiko has asked are very detailed, so detailed and so asked at such a rate that I was unable to write them down. If possible, please send them in writing. We will also make a detailed reply in writing. Thank you very much. On the other hand, if I can relate in such a general way, then I will only say that the temporary application of the agreement will exclude those entities, objects which belong to the exclusive competence of the European Union. This will apply to this temporary use of agri-food products. This period of provisional application will be used to assess the possible impact this agreement will have on our agriculture or our food economy. I just want to say in general that despite some general reservations in our assessment there will be no direct threat to Poland's agricultural sector. This is mainly due to several reasons. Firstly, Canada has a small share of agricultural imports of the Union and Poland, and the liberalisation of customs duties in imports of the most sensitive agricultural products from Canada will be limited. With regard to what you are most concerned about, namely food safety, all veterinary and sanitary conditions will have to be met on an equivalence basis, actually compliance, because there is a difference. Nothing that has so far been prohibited as regards the placing on the market of the European Union will be allowed after the entry into force of this agreement. In short, goods which have not yet been authorised on the Union market will still not be allowed. With regard to the further argument that, however, we are saying that this will not have a major impact on our agricultural sector, we also see some advantages in the case of processed fruit and vegetables, because we will be able to find a market. As far as checks are concerned, everything will be done on the basis of the rules so far. All inspections that have been carried out will be combined into a single inspection, the State Food Safety Inspection, which will carry out checks at border crossing points, at the importer's premises, at the destination. It depends on the kind of food. In any case, these control rules, the choice of goods for inspection are based on EU and national rules, but mostly EU rules. As regards goods from Canada, some simplifications result from an agreement between the European Union and Canada, and in no way do these simplifications jeopardise food safety. During Mr. Andriukaitis' last visit in May Ub. R. such a question has been raised as regards the requirements for food safety that we will apply to simplifications or derogations. The Commissioner clearly said that there are no concessions or compromises when it comes to food. Requirements that are laid down in the European Union will be imposed on food originating in Canada. I would like to say that, of course, the frequency of controls and goods are set out in the relevant regulations and decisions of the European Commission, which are directly applicable, and that is what we apply. If an example were to be given for mandatory checks at a certain frequency, it would be envisaged, for example. flaxseed, since flaxseed was found to be a GMO product in 2009. Therefore, flaxseed originating in that area shall be inspected. It should be noted that no non-compliance has been found since 2011. In short, when it comes to controls, there is no risk that they will not be applied. That's one thing. Second, however, all the requirements that are made during the production of food in Poland and the European Union will also be made. That is what I would like to say at the outset, but the answers to these specific questions will be given to you in writing. Thank you very much.

Many Poles do not go to a doctor, but heal themselves by buying dietary supplements. Last year was record for the number of new dietary supplements on the market. They were introduced 7400 thousand. This reality shows that state regulations should not allow such unfair practices to be applied. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u61	Thank you. And the Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u62	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, of course, the Ministry of Health recognises the problem or problems raised by the applicants. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, we must also remember that Poland is a Member State of the European Union and this also applies to this segment of the market as regards dietary supplements. EU regulations are in some respects... Of course, they also regulate the Polish market, are superior to Polish law and limit our ability to act, but I will present to you what actions and projects the Minister of Health is preparing. I will also read this sentence: However, it should be pointed out that the judgments of the European Court of Justice clearly indicate that, in the absence of grounds for confirming the risk to human health and life, the placing on the market of such a product cannot be blocked. Of course, dietary supplements are not allowed on the market, but it is simply a notification or a notification to the chief health inspector. So it's food. Supplements are food and are subject to food law. Nevertheless, ladies and gentlemen, we see here the need for regulation. Poles actually abuse dietary supplements. In fact, supplements, as the name suggests, should only be used if there is a missing element or a substance in the body. In fact, in today's world, such food shortages are extremely rare, in fact in the course of serious diseases or as a result of serious treatment, e.g. chemotherapy when treating debilitating diseases. In fact, at the moment we know that supplements behave like drugs, and therefore their sales and demand for these substances can be created by aggressive marketing. Of course, we see this dependence and this dependence is certain, because the more money is spent on marketing, the greater is the consumption of a given supplement. Of course we also see other pathological phenomena, i.e. contamination of these supplements, as indicated in the report of the NIK and as it was already underlined. But we also see that e.g. in one supplement is, let's say, a full postulated dose of this substance, which should be delivered within 24 hours for man, and this supplement costs 20 zł, while at this point a supplement that contains 1/2 doses is sold in terms of human daily demand, and costs twice as much, costs 40 zł. Of course, this preparation, which contains less substances, is sold only as a result of marketing, and the higher price allows the manufacturer to conduct more aggressive marketing. This, ladies and gentlemen, is, in my opinion and in the opinion of the Ministry, incorrect actions. Now about what we're planning. In fact, it is already prepared in the Ministry of Health a major pharmaceutical law amendment project. We are waiting for a decision on the parliamentary project at the moment. What, on the other hand, did we propose in this project? We are considering introducing fees for the notification of the placing on the market of dietary supplements and for changing these notifications - to the chief health inspector. Of course, we want to regulate advertising, i.e. advertising must not contain false content. So we don't want what the media often writes about, we don't want to restrict advertising, but we want to eliminate false content from advertising so that consumers are not bombarded with false information. We want to reduce the range of dietary supplements in pharmacies. This means that this preparation - this is the example that I presented to you - I think this more expensive preparation containing less of this substance, which is not proposed, should not be marketed in the pharmacy. Come on. Of course, we want supplements to be in some way easily distinguishable by consumers, i.e. distinguishable from medicinal products, and we will introduce, we will postulate the introduction of blue belt on all pack supplements. This appearance must be clearly different from that of medicinal products. Of course, we also want to limit the possibility of using the so-called. brands or umbrella names that mimic medicines. We want to introduce the need to study dietary supplements before they are put into the sale to pharmacies. We also want to ban the use of such tricks in advertising, that it is a doctor or a person who looks like a doctor who recommends the use of a given supplement, and introduce an absolute ban on advertising during a children's programme. These are the things we can, of course, introduce. I will tell you that the last positive thing is, I think, that the producer community itself sees these irregularities and at the moment introduces a code of ethics itself, subjecting itself to self-regulation. We do not yet know whether this code will be effective and whether this [[♪ The bell ♪]] Self-regulation will be effective or not. Thank you very much.

This draft law provides for a committee to be set up to coordinate and initiate activities related to these ceremonial celebrations. The Committee, in line with the President's proposal, would, of course, be composed of a representative of his firm, ministers who would somehow participate in organising these events, including the coordinator of the Council of Ministers appointed for this purpose, but also a wide range of people representing the various environments appointed by the President. Two others, which sought to expand the composition of Parliament's representation, and to extend it to those who held office in the past, were ultimately not adopted. During the committee's work there was a correction concerning the financing of the Committee's activities by the President of the Republic of Poland and the Ministry of Culture. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u214	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice towards the draft Act on the National Obstetrics of the Hundredth Anniversary of the Reclaim of Independence of the Republic of Poland presented by the President of the Republic of Poland. The Law and Justice Club welcomes this initiative by President Andrzej Duda, because such a unique event, or actually a whole series of major events, which resulted in Poland regaining independence after a hundred decades of partitions and captivity, deserves a solemn and well-organized celebration, which should refer to the whole course of the years of the struggle for freedom and then its defence, especially against the Bolshevik upheaval. It is necessary to remind the efforts of several generations of Poles, first of all trying to save the Republic through the Bar Confederation, the Constitution of 3 May, the war in its defence with the Moskals and the uprising of Kościuszkowskie, then multiple independence raids, the Legion of Dąbrowski, the Duchy of Warsaw, the Napoleonic Empire with the figure of Prince Józef Poniatowski, the November Uprising, the Kraków Uprising, the fight in the Spring of the Peoples, the January Uprising with the steadfast Romuald Traugutt and the often forgotten Baikal Uprising. The sacrifice of health and life of many hundreds of thousands of patriots obliges the next generations to remember and care for the Republic. In a special way we should celebrate the action of this generation, to which independence was given: from the secret circles of self-education, the Union of Action, the Strzelecki Union, the Polish Shooting Teams, the Sokolich Field Teams, the Bartosz Teams and the Polish Scouting Teams, through the fights of the Polish Legions of Chief Józef Piłsudski, the Puławy Legion, the Polish Corpses in the East, the Polish Army in France, and the shed blood of hundreds of thousands of Poles forced to serve in the army of conquerors, to the defense of Lviv and Małopolska East, to the uprising of Greater Poland, three Silesian uprises and effective defence only in the long-term, war-growing area and bloody war with Bolsheviks. It was they who, heirs of the Confederates and insurgents, conspirators and sibiraks, who led their actions to regain the freedom of our homeland. It is worth recalling during the next celebrations the figures of the leaders of the armed struggle as well as the civil, organizational and diplomatic effort: Józef Piłsudski, Roman Dmowski, Ignacy Jan Paderewski, Wincenty Witos, Wojciech Korfante, Ignacy Daszyński, Kazimierz Sosnkowski, Józef Haller, Józef Dowbor-Muśnicki, Maciej Rataj, Maurycy Zamoyski, Feliks Młynarski, Valery Sławek, heroic priests from Fr. Ignatius Skorupka at the head, but also all the participants in these activities, especially the soldiers who gave their lives for the independence of their homeland. To initiate and carry out several years of celebrations that will start this year and will last until 2021, the Committee of the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of Recovering Independence of the Republic of Poland will be very helpful and necessary, whose appointment was proposed by the President. The Committee will adopt an annual plan of celebrations, promote the idea of commemorating regaining independence in Poland and abroad, initiate cooperation between all stakeholders involved in the celebrations, express an opinion on the patronage of the President of the Republic of Poland over individual celebrations, and coordinate and inspire a number of regional celebrations. The committee will consist of the highest state authorities, representatives of parties and political environments and many people particularly worthy of the Polish State, which will be appointed by the President of the Republic of Poland. The Law and Justice Club explicitly supports the bill submitted and at the same time submits two amendments clarifying the law and ensuring the smooth operation of the committee also after the forthcoming elections to the Polish Sejm. First amendment to Article 2 (2) Point 3 (4) consists of replacing the dot with a semicolon and adding the following point 5: upon completion of the tasks, the Committee shall adopt a report on the activities of the Committee. The amendment aims to clearly indicate in the provisions of the Act concerning the tasks of the Committee that one of them is the adoption of a report on the Committee's activities after completion of the tasks related to the celebrations. The second amendment consists in adding an article. 5a as follows: 1. Membership of the committee of persons referred to in Article Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 2, expires on the date of the beginning of the term of office of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. The provisions of the Article shall apply. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Article 2 and Article 2 Point 5 (1) shall apply mutatis mutandis. 2. The authorised entities shall, within 21 days of the beginning of the term of office of the Sejm, provide the President of the Republic of Poland with information about the representative appointed to act as a member of the committee or shall make a declaration of resignation. Failure to submit information or a statement shall be tantamount to giving up participation in the Committee's work. The point is that the committee can continue to function smoothly after the parliamentary elections. [[♪ The bell ♪]] I'm handing this over to the marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u273	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft Act on the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of the Recovering of Independence of the Republic of Poland presented by the President of the Republic of Poland, prints 1093 and 1272. The project assumes the creation of a Committee of the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of Recovering Independence of the Republic of Poland composed of the highest authorities, representatives of political parties and groups, representatives of churches and other religious associations, trade unions, organizations of combatants and other social organizations or people particularly worthy of the Polish State. To this group, by means of an amendment, the Committee on Culture and Media has rightly added representatives of cultural and scientific circles, who will certainly support the work of the Committee with their authority and substance. The establishment of the committee aims to guarantee the celebration of the 100th anniversary of Poland's regaining independence of the highest possible rank in order to commemorate this extremely important anniversary of events from 1918, as well as events from subsequent years, during which independence was preserved throughout the entire territory of the Republic of Poland, such as the Polish-Bolshevik War, the Wielkopolska Uprising or the three Silesian Uprisings. The importance of organising the 100th anniversary of regaining independence by the Republic, in which representatives of all political groups, social organisations and state and local authorities would take part over political divisions, is borne out by the conclusions of the study of the National Centre for Culture conducted by TNS Polska, which was included in the presentation of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage presented by Minister Jarosław Sellin at the meeting of the Parliamentary Group for Dignity, Community and Inclusion in 2018 of the 100th anniversary of Polish Independence Recovery which took place on 1 December 2016. By the way, Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle have joined the envoy. by declaring personal participation in the preparation of the 100th anniversary. The most important conclusion in our assessment, mentioned in the program rather not by accident as the first one, is: In Poland there is a lack of sense of a strong community. Poles still feel that they share more than they do. It is precisely the efforts to strengthen the sense of the civic community that should be a priority for the activities of public authorities, including our parliamentary work. The responsibility lies with the government of the Republic, but also with the local government. The location of the transfer investment may be included in the study of the conditions and directions of spatial development of the municipality, in the local spatial planning plan of the municipality or in the decision on the conditions of construction and land development, building permit. The localisation decision gives legal title to the use of real estate for construction purposes. The Act aims to increase the level of security of power supply to urban agglomerations. Strategic investments in transmission networks are public objectives within the meaning of the Real Estate Management Act of 21 August 1997. The main benefits of applying the provisions of the Act currently in force are the limitation of the number of permits necessary for the implementation of the investment - a comprehensive decision to determine the location of investments in the transmission network linking the aspects of the localisation, division and expropriation decision - a concentration of competence to issue a localisation decision and a building permit in the hands of one authority, in this case the voivodeships of the region in which the longest segment of the investment is located, shortening the duration of the procedures necessary to obtain the required decisions, the rigour of immediate feasibility for the decision issued at first instance. Unfortunately, such a procedure means, at the same time, a limitation of civil rights, which I mentioned at the outset. The wording of the Article may confirm the radical limitation of these rights. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u313	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the law on the preparation and implementation of strategic investments in the field of transmission networks, print no. 1257. The proposed amendment to the Law of 24 July 2015 on the preparation and implementation of strategic investments in transmission networks aims to extend the list of strategic investments in transmission networks set out in the Annex to the Act, which are of key importance in ensuring the energy security of the country. Applicants point to the need to carry out these tasks mainly because of the construction of a new 1075 MW carbon block in Kozienice Power Plant, which increases the installed capacity of the power plant by more than 30%. The investment will allow power to be removed from the power plant, which will increase the power transmission capacity towards Warsaw and the north-eastern part of the country with all the criteria for reliable operation of the network. This will have a positive impact on the development of the Warsaw agglomeration and the north-eastern part of Poland, which is included in deficit areas in terms of coverage of power and electricity demand due to the small number of generation sources. In addition, the investment will partially increase the level of security of power supply for the Krakow agglomeration. According to the applicants, the procedated special act should lead to a reduction in the number of administrative decisions taken due to the optimisation of the process of issuing them by excluding certain provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure in scope. Moreover, the revenue of the state budget and local government units is expected to increase as a result of the increase in income from PIT, CIT, VAT and revenue from local taxes and charges. It is also important that these strategic investments in the construction of electricity transmission lines will not be financed by the state budget, which means that this regulation will not affect the public finance sector. It is also important that the conditions for stable economic development of the country will be created by improving the quality and reliability of electricity supply and thus the development of transmission networks will directly contribute to the increase in the volume and scope of production of local enterprises. I am convinced that these factors will compensate for any inconvenience to the inhabitants of the areas through which the electricity lines will run. In the view of the applicants, this regulation will give the regions instruments to make better use of their assets, including the use of EU funds. Regions will increase their competitiveness in attracting investors and will have additional opportunities to shape the labour market. This in turn will positively boost entrepreneurship and employment through the development of existing and the emergence of new economic operators. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u315	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media on the work on the parliamentary draft resolution on the commemoration of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki on the 30th anniversary of his death. During the work of the commission, Robert Telus presented the biography and importance of the work of God's servant Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki, who contributed to the consolidation of the most important social attitudes of Poles. On 2 December 2016, Draft Resolution No 1099 entered the Sejm and was then addressed to the first reading in the Committee on Culture and Media, where it was discussed in detail. The Committee on Culture and Media adopted the resolution unanimously. Father Franciszek Blaclinicki was born on 24 March 1921 in Rybnik. During World War II he fought with the Nazi invader and stayed in the concentration camp in Oświęcim for 14 months, of which he spent 9 months in the penal company. In March 1942, he was sentenced to death by beheading for conspiracy against the Nazi Reich. After more than 4.5 months of waiting for the execution of the sentence, he was pardoned, and the death penalty was replaced with 10 years in prison after the end of the war. During his stay in the convict's unit, Francis Blaclinicki's sudden, miraculous conversion to personal faith in Christ, combined with his decision to give his life in his service, was accomplished. After the end of the war, he joined the Silesian Seminary in Krakow. He organized and ran a catechetical centre, and since 1957 - social anti-alcoholic action Mon. The Crusade of Abstinence, which took on the character of a religious-moral renewal movement based on spirituality o. Maximilian Kolby. In 1963 Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki re-run the Oase Light-Life Retreat. The beginnings of the history of the movement go back to the first oasis, i.e. the retreat of a retreat of a survival character, which took place in 1954 with the participation of altar boys. The formation work started in the retreat oasis was continued in a small group in the parish. Father Franciszek Blaclinicki as the founder of the movement has developed the necessary assistance for it. In this way the oasis has developed into a movement called the Light-Life Movement, which aims to raise mature Christians. Despite various external difficulties, the Light-Life Movement has developed very quickly in Poland and has penetrated beyond Poland - Bulgaria, Brazil, Lithuania, the Czech Republic, Germany, Austria, it is also present in Belarus, Ukraine and Latvia since 2006 - Ireland and the United Kingdom, as well as in the United States, China and Canada. It currently operates in more than 30 countries, making it an international movement. Father Franciszek Blaclinicki died suddenly in Carlsberg on 27 February 1987. On 9 December 1995, his beatification process began. He left a rich scientific, popular and scientific and ascetic-formative achievements. On 17 February 1994, he was honoured posthumously with the Commander's Cross of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland, and on 5 May 1995 he received the Auschwitz Cross posthumously. The 30th anniversary of the death of God's servant Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki is a great event in the history of Poland. His life and activities throughout the period of the Polish People's Republic show that he was not only a holy priest, but also a great patriot, who was an educator in the freedom of Poles. In view of the above, the Committee on Culture and Media calls for the commemoration of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki on the 30th anniversary of his death. As I said at the beginning, the committee unanimously adopted a resolution of the Polish Parliament on the commemoration of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki on the 30th anniversary of his death: On the 30th anniversary of the death of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki, Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to this great patriot, soldier, clergyman and social activist. Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki devoted his entire life to the welfare of his homeland. He fought as a soldier in the September campaign, acted in conspiracy, was imprisoned in a concentration camp, persecuted by communist security services. His social activities were based on universal values, shaped the moral attitudes of the nation. As the creator of the Light - Life Movement, he raised young people and adults in the spirit of patriotism, morality and responsibility. Sejm of the Republic of Poland - appreciating the merits of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki for the Polish Nation and his contribution to the moral development of many generations - he pays tribute to his memory. So much for a resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Wojtyła, who protected the movement of oazowa from the authorities of the Polish People's Republic. Balnicki established an Independent Christian Social Service to promote the idea of a nonviolent liberation movement. The martial law found him in Rome, he never returned to Poland again. Balnicki went to prison and was tried for his struggle to liberate his country from drunkenness. Interestingly, he was tried in the same room where the Germans sentenced him to death for fighting for the liberation of Poland 20 years ago. Father Blaclinicki started his studies and work at the Catholic University of Lublin, where he received a PhD and habilitation, twice not approved by the state authorities for political reasons. He developed scientific and pastoral work throughout the country, building a centered Oasis Movement of the Living Church, which was called the Movement of Light-Life, and whose branch is also the Home Church. In this activity he was particularly supported by cardinals. Wojtyła and later Pope John Paul II. Retreat oasiss in Poland included the evangelization of hundreds of thousands of children, young people, adults and whole families, which today results in social responsibility. Blaclinicki along with the Movement of Light-Life, inspired by the teaching of John Paul II, initiated a great movement to build a culture of free life - the Crusade of the Liberation of Man raising up to sobriety and social-political engagement. The next social works of Father Blaclinicki were related to the breakthrough of Solidarity - evangelization of working environments and the Independent Christian Social Service established in 1981. The martial law was found by Fr. Blaclinicki on his trip to a foreign symposium. Seeing the great pastoral needs of the emigration of martial law, he settled in a Polish center in Carlsberg, creating a center for evangelization, doing apostolic work there. He established the Christian Service for the Liberation of Nations, uniting old and new emigration. He collaborated with emigration from other Eastern European countries, thus preparing a post-Sovieticum overcoming the post-Sovietist order. In this way he created the trend of Polish liberation theology, which was part of the world's non-violence movement, which means liberation without violence. Father Blaclinicki was under surveillance by a multiplayer specifically established in Nowy Sącz - it is estimated that more than 200 officers - the SB cell, also abroad, and as a wanted by the martial law authorities with a BOLO, which was only cancelled in 1995, had a closed return to the country. He died suddenly in 1987. And here one of the Members also pointed out that there are many doubts about his death in Carlsberg, because it turned out that the closest family, the marriage that worked with Fr. Balnicki, they were security officers. His pastoral initiatives also bring fruit in social life today, among others. By raising free people. His beatification process started in 1995 in Katowice led to the announcement in Rome on 1 October 2015 of a decree on the heroicity of virtues constituting the basic stage of the future beatification. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u337	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers and some other laws contained in print no. 1274. The drafting of the bill submitted due to the legal arrangements which are important for the functioning of the labour market and the reduction of irregularities in both the functioning of the employment agencies and temporary employment agencies, the employment of temporary workers or outsourcing was preceded by extensive consultations with the social partners and other stakeholders active in the temporary labour market. These consultations have recently been held at the Social Dialogue Council in the Labour Law Problem Team. The findings resulting from the work of this team have completed a period of more than 2 years of various activities on the analysis of the requests made and the effects of their possible introduction into the legal order and on the temporary labour market. The social partners involved in the consultation considered it appropriate to undertake specific legislative work in the areas covered by the draft law, indicating that this would be an effective means of achieving the intended objective of raising the standards of temporary work and combating abuse in this area. I would like to stress that many of the solutions adopted in the draft law are the result of an agreement or compromise between representatives of environments interested in the development of the temporary labour market in Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Temporary employment contracts are seen in the labour market as a very flexible and relatively low financial burden on the employment model. The result is an increasing use of this form of work by companies and a rapid, continuous development of the temporary labour market. The number of temporary employment agencies and users' companies is increasing and the number of temporary workers is increasing. Unfortunately, at the same time there is information about the abuse of this form of employment and the non-compliance with the rights of temporary workers. The draft law is therefore intended to introduce regulations to improve the employment conditions of temporary workers, to increase the protection of the employment agency's clients, as well as the legal security of temporary employment agencies and employers of users and, as a result, to improve the standards of temporary employment. The legal solutions proposed in the proposed bill should also improve the effectiveness of the control activities carried out by the State Labour Inspection in the area of broadly understood temporary work. I will now come to the discussion of the most important solutions contained in the bill. They relate in particular to the provisions of the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers and the Act on the Promotion of Employment and Employment Market Institutions. The key proposal from the point of view of the proposed law is to specify precisely in the provisions of the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers the limits of temporary employment, the current arrangements in this respect. they are insufficient and do not prevent the creation of successive temporary agency only for the purpose of ensuring that the same temporary worker can be repeatedly directed to the performance of temporary work for the same employer of the user. Such a phenomenon is often called the doughnuting of temporary employment agencies. The operation of such agencies in practice leads to a deterioration of the substance of temporary work, making the institution for the temporary use of a person's work a model based on the continuous use of such work in cases where the work should and may be carried out by its own employees of the user's employer. The project therefore assumes a limit of 18 months of temporary work carried out over a period of 36 consecutive calendar months, both in the case of directing the same temporary worker to the user's employer concerned, and in the case of the use of the same temporary worker by a specific employer. This limit will therefore apply regardless of whether the referral to the employer is made from one or more temporary employment agencies. This limit will also apply to the performance of temporary work under civil law agreements, as well as where a person performs such work under both a contract of employment and a contract of civil law. I hope that the proposed regulation on the limits of temporary work will reduce the phenomenon of the agency's doughnuting and thus restore the proper, temporary nature of such work. Ensuring the proper application of the proposed rules on temporary work limits requires the introduction of arrangements enabling the temporary agency to determine whether the applicant has been working for the user's employer in the last 36 calendar months, on the basis of which and for how long. This will be served by the proposed amendments to the provisions on the content of the work certificates issued by the temporary agency, as well as new arrangements for the issuing of certificates by such agencies on the performance of temporary work by the person concerned on the basis of civil law agreements. It is also important to propose a change, according to which the temporary staff member will have much easier contact with the temporary agency employing him. At present such contact is difficult, because the temporary worker most often does not know where and how he could contact his legal employer, the agency of temporary employment. Because of the specific nature of the temporary work, he keeps in constant contact with the employer of the user, testifying to his work. The draft therefore requires the temporary agency to provide the temporary staff member with contact details to the agency that employs him and to provide information on any modification of such data. Such a solution should improve the legal situation of temporary workers and enable them to establish direct contact with the agency, e.g. if necessary, explain the current concerns about the work they provide. Another major change in the provisions of the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers is the proposal that the contract of employment with a pregnant temporary worker should be extended until the date of birth if such contract is to be terminated after the expiry of the third month of pregnancy. I admit that during the drafting of the bill, this solution was controversial, although the proposal to cover such workers with specific legal protection was not contested. We have sought different alternatives, taking into account in particular the organisational and financial consequences of extending the contract until the date of birth, which may arise on the part of temporary employment agencies and employers of users. I think that a fair assessment of the new rules will only be possible after a certain period of time. The draft submitted also provides for amendments to the rules on how to calculate the remuneration for temporary staff leave periods and the monetary equivalent for such leave. The need to clarify these provisions was notified by all interested parties to the temporary labour market as well as by the State Labour Inspection. The specific nature of temporary work justifies the application in this respect of certain exceptions in relation to the regulations covered by workers engaged in work under the Labour Code. However, these exceptions should be precisely enough to ensure that, in practice, there are no doubts as to how to calculate the remuneration for the temporary worker's rest period or the amount of his equivalent for such leave. The proposed law contains such regulations. New regulations determining the criminal liability of both temporary employment agencies and the employer of the user for violating certain provisions of the Act also serve to increase the effectiveness of the checks carried out by the State Labour Inspection. This concerns, in particular, non-compliance with the rules on temporary work limits and the assignment of temporary work to a worker who cannot be carried out by him. It is also proposed to extend the liability of the user's employer to cases of failure to keep records of the working time of the temporary worker and to prevent temporary use of rest leave. In order to increase the effectiveness of the proposed solutions and to not differentiate sanctions against employers of users and temporary employment agencies for offences against the rights of temporary staff, the proposed law provides for a fine to be fixed at the same level as laid down in the Labour Code for offences against workers' rights, i.e. 1 000. PLN up to 30,000. zł. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed bill also contains amendments to the Employment Promotion Act and the Employment Market Institutions concerning the employment agencies' activities. The proposed amendments aim to increase the level of protection of persons using employment agencies' services and to improve the effectiveness of the State Labour Inspection's control activities in the fight against abuse and pathology in the field of the employment agencies' activities. To this end, two types of employment agencies have been introduced: temporary employment services and employment services, personal advice and career guidance. Both types of agencies will receive different certificates. Secondly, an obligation to register employment agencies for foreign entrepreneurs from the European Union providing temporary work has been introduced. The adoption of such a solution will increase the level of protection of persons using temporary employment services, as a number of obligations and sanctions relating to failure to fulfil these obligations are imposed on the employment agency, together with the entry in the employment agency's register. The furthest and most severe sanction is the removal of the employment agency from the above mentioned. the register, which is equivalent to the prohibition on the establishment of employment agencies. Thirdly, there is an obligation to certify the service of directing to work foreigners to entities operating in Poland and new rules for the provision of this service. Such a solution is intended to prevent the current pathology of the labour market by importing and employing foreign nationals by illegal employment agencies. Employment agencies will be required, inter alia. to conclude a written contract with a foreigner addressed to work, informing the foreigner and the entity to which the foreigner is directed to work, among others. about the rules concerning the residence and work of foreigners in Poland, keeping a list of entities to which foreigners are directed, and the list of foreigners to work. Fourthly, the Marshal of the voivodship was given the opportunity to check, every quarter, the arrears of employment agencies providing employment services for temporary social security contributions for employees. The verification will be carried out through the electronic systems set up and made available by the Minister responsible for Labour, using the electronic system of the Social Insurance Institution. Fifthly, the obligation to have an establishment for the provision of employment agency services has been restored, as requested by the State Labour Inspection. Sixthly, the scope of data submitted by employment agencies has been extended, among others. a consequence of the introduction of a separate service for the directing to work of foreigners. After the seventh, there was an obligation for the Marshals of voivodships to keep a list of foreign entrepreneurs from the countries of the European Union providing services in the field of employment mediation, personal advice or vocational guidance in Poland - within the framework of a notification to the Marshal of the voivodship. After eight o'clock, higher penalties were imposed for illegal service provided by employment agencies and for violations of the law against persons employed by them or employed as temporary workers - from 3 000. Up to 100,000. PLN depending on the type of service of the employment agency - and penalties have been imposed for failing to fulfil the new obligations imposed by the employment agency. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We are assuming a relatively rapid entry into force of the proposed changes, as in principle the new rules would start to apply on 1 June this year. It is important that on the temporary labour market it is necessary to bring about the application and compliance of new legislation on the employment of temporary workers as soon as possible, including on the limits of temporary work. The rapid application of new legal solutions is also intended to be subject to transitional provisions, according to which new solutions will be applied in principle to temporary employment contracts on the date of entry into force of the Act. This will also apply to the limits of temporary work. We assume that the period of temporary work carried out on the basis of contracts on the date of the entry into force of the Act, starting on that date, would be included in the limits of the temporary work provided for in the new provisions. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! A detailed description of the changes is contained in the explanatory memorandum of the draft together with the impact assessment and the consultation report of the social partners. I therefore request that the draft law on the amendment of the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers and certain other laws, contained in print No. 1274, be referred to for further work in the Sejm committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Quote from the explanatory memorandum of the 2003 project: In the light of the entire regulation, the temporary employment agency will be able to carry out job placement tasks for temporary workers only as their employer within the meaning of the Labour Code. On the other hand, the temporary employment agency will be able to carry out job placement tasks as a party to a contract of employment or a civil law contract concluded with them in relation to persons aged 16 and 26 who are pupils or students and to persons who receive a pension. Another quote from the explanatory memorandum: The proposed law provides for a contract of employment as a basis for a legal relationship between a temporary worker and a temporary employment agency. Alternative application of the civil law agreement would only be possible for pupils, students, pensioners and pensioners. After the amendment, the law of civil law contracts was limited only to persons aged 16 to 18 years. What's the conclusion? The purpose of the Act on the Employment of Temporary Workers was not to allow them to be recruited under civil law agreements. The mere fact that the agencies are doing this is because the 2003 Act is vague and does not contain a provision which would prohibit it directly. It must be said out loud - people employed in temporary employment agencies are very often people who are unable to find themselves in the labour market, are inefficient, move in a thicket of bureaucracy and regulations. They want to work and earn money, they often do not realize how unfavorable it is for them to enter into civil law contracts. For me, the provision you intend to introduce into the article. 7th bill, sounds funny. How do you want to allow a temporary employment agency under a civil law contract to direct non-workers of such an agency to do work, even if it is temporary work? Temporary work is a special type of work done for the employer of the user and can only be carried out in the three cases indicated in the act, but remains the work. How can you hire an employee to do work under a civil law contract? The fact that the State Labour Inspection does not properly carry out its tasks does not entitle us to legalize what has so far been an act of infradem legis. We are only able to agree to legalise the conclusion of civil law contracts by the agencies if the performance of the contractor or contractor is not a job, but will actually be a commission or a work, while you know very well how abused both forms are. Each of us has a family or friends person who worked in the garbage can who provided work under a civil law contract. No paid leave, no minimum wage, etc., etc. Is this really what we want to condemn Poles who, in view of the complex situation in the labour market, are looking for employment in temporary employment agencies? ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u384	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a resolution of the Senate of the Republic of Poland of 11 January 2017 on the submission to the Sejm of a draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure and the Law on proceedings before administrative courts. The draft law is the implementation of the request made in the petition P9/23/16 submitted to the Senate on 21 March 2016. The petitioner asked for an amendment to the article. 95 of the Law of 30 August 2002. The law on proceedings before administrative courts, which states that only adults are allowed to enter the courtroom in public, apart from the parties and the persons called, and that only the persons called are allowed access to confidential meetings. The petitioner proposed adding to the article. 95 Law on proceedings before administrative courts § 2 as follows: The Court of First Instance may authorise the presence at a public hearing of a minor who has completed 13. A year old. The Senate of the Republic of Poland adopted a draft amendment to the Law on the Law of Procedure before administrative courts without limitation of the age in which a minor, with the consent of the chairman of the formation of the court, could be present at a public hearing. The draft law establishes the possibility for minors to attend the hearing without specifying the lower age limit. Such a solution ensures the freedom of decision of the President, who will take it individually, taking into account inter alia. the smooth course of the meeting or the possible demoralisation of the minor by the content presented during the meeting. In addition, the provision is intended to sanction the practice of giving parents with young children permission to enter the courtroom. It was fully justified by the Higher Chamber that the Code of Civil Procedure should be amended in the same way, since it would be unreasonable to regulate this matter differently in civil and judicial procedures. At this point, it should be added that, under the current criminal procedure, the rules do not limit the participation of minors in legal proceedings where cases are much more drastic and dramatic than in civil and judicial proceedings. High Seym! The proposed amendments lead to the implementation of one of the fundamental principles and constitutional guarantees, namely the principle of transparency of hearings, which has been guaranteed in the Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution and Article. 6 the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. Publicity of proceedings means that a court hearing is generally accessible to the public and, in principle, everyone has the right to enter the courtroom. The public's presence during the proceedings is intended to ensure that public scrutiny of the manner in which decisions given by the court are to be processed and enforced. Exclusion of disclosure in accordance with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Constitution may take place because of the morality, security of the state and public order and because of the protection of the private life of the parties or other important private interest. Minors shall have the right to have an opinion. They should know how judicial authority works, which will contribute to shaping their own views. Young people are extremely active players in public life, are interested in social activities, take initiatives for the local environment. The educational value is not without significance for the proposed change. Many matters important to the community are settled in Polish courts. Allowing minors to enter the courtroom will prepare them for active participation in public life. The project was sent to the following parties for an opinion: the Minister of Justice, the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court, the National Judicial Council, the Association of Polish Judges, the National Council of Legal Counsels, the Supreme Bar Council, the National Council of Curators and the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. The Minister of Justice's opinion was positive. The Supreme Administrative Court and the National Judicial Board have not submitted comments on the draft. In his opinion, the Supreme Court pointed out that children's access to court should be restricted for their benefit. In addition, the presence of children in the courtroom undermines the seriousness of the court and may affect the quality of the explanations or testimony of the parent. According to the Supreme Court, a minor should have access to the courtroom after a certain age. The opinion identifies an example of 16 years of age. Further work on the law according to the Supreme Court should be preceded by the opinion of specialists in development psychology. I'll admit I'm a little surprised by this opinion and this radical opposition from the Supreme Court to the presence of minors in court. I don't think there are things happening in courtrooms in civil and administrative matters that are so shocking that they threaten the trauma and demoralization of minors. He also wonders that the Supreme Court challenged the preparation of Polish judges to assess the state of mental development and emotional maturity of the youth. The other requested entities did not submit their views on the project. She presented her opinion on the project to the Public Prosecutor General of the State Treasury, who did not comment, and to the Ombudsman of the rights of the child who assessed the project positively. The proposed vacathio legis of this law is 14 days. 87 senators took part in the Senate vote, 85 of which voted in favour of the project and 2 abstained. No one was against it. I ask the High Sejm to adopt the draft law proposed by the Senate in the proposed wording. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u399	Thank you, Member. Mr Marek Michalak asked for the opportunity to speak. You're welcome, Mr. Spokesman.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u400	Dear Marshal, Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, At the outset, it should be pointed out that the draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code and the Law on the Procedure of Administrative Courts, which has been adopted by the High Chamber today, introduces a new quality of participation of children in public life. The source of inspiration for the project was the individual petition submitted by Tymon Radzik, a student of middle school in Zielona Góra. It concerned the possibility for minors to participate in public hearings in administrative courts. According to Art. 95 Rights of legal proceedings before administrative courts have the right to participate in a public hearing, apart from the parties and persons called upon, only adults. Thus, minors are not allowed to enter the courtroom and cannot observe the proceedings unless, of course, they are either a party or a person called by the court. The view expressed by the teenage petitioner should be shared: there are a lot of minors who are interested in social activity, civil society and the promotion of their local environment and even the whole country. Many matters relevant to the community are dealt with in administrative courts which shape the legal situation in public administration proceedings. On the other hand, I would merely mention that the Ombudsman referred in two cases to the Supreme Administrative Court cassation actions for decisions issued by the voivodship administrative courts. These cases concerned infringement of the right of the author of the petition to have access to public information. These facts point to an increasing awareness of their own subjectivity, their civic involvement, their willingness to co-create social and economic life and their active participation in public life. Let me remind you that human rights begin with the rights of a child. The right of the child is therefore among others. the right to information about how power works and the right to shape its own views. It is also part of the right to information, to have opinions and freedom of speech. I am pleased to note that today's draft amendment to the Civil Procedure Code and the Law on the Procedure of Administrative Courts does not only meet this petition, but fills the legal gap - the right of the child to participate in judicial proceedings in civil matters on the rights of the public. In view of the above, the Ombudsman supports the project in its entirety. It gives minors the right to be present in public at public hearings in respect of civil proceedings before a general court and in administrative matters decided by an administrative court. This strengthens the subjectivity of the child, enabling active participation in public life, gaining knowledge and experience in civil and administrative matters that are of interest to him. This promotes respect for the law and strengthens the sense of justice in children. The proposed change is the implementation of the child's right to information. This law shapes the development of the child, is important to meet his educational needs, his schooling obligation or his preparation for active life in civil society. It is also the exercise of the right of the child to participate, free expression of his own views and the right to be heard, which allows the minor to participate in forms of social activity such as. activities in youth councils, participation in public consultations or participation in public dialogue. In the view of the Ombudsman, the proposal rightly does not introduce an age restriction for a minor. The President of the proceedings, allowing a minor to be present at a public court hearing, will assess whether the access of the minor to the court is intended to exercise the right of the child to information and to freely formulate opinions on the action of the judicial authority and to develop his own views on whether he or she infringes the interests of the child or undermines the seriousness of the court and whether he or she can influence the quality of the explanations or testimony of the persons heard in the proceedings. It should be stressed that the proposed regulation is in line with the implementation of the provisions of the Convention on the Rights of the Child and the recommendations of the United Nations Children's Rights Committee of 29 October 2015. It is also compatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Not only does it complete the legal regulations in force in the Polish criminal procedure for almost 50 years, but it refines them. For these reasons, the draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code and the Law on proceedings before administrative courts in the assessment of the Ombudsman deserves to be welcomed and accepted, as I also ask your Members. It is always worth creating a law that does not only have a preventive function, but also, not less important, educational. It is particularly important to create such a law for young citizens. Young people are paying close attention to the functioning of the state, and some who are good and important want to participate actively in this process. Janusz Korczak, speaking of children, pointed to the right direction, whether it was wise or not. The Polish legislator must not forget this. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u406	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members' Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour to present our position on the Senate draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act and the Law on proceedings before administrative courts. The aim of this project is to allow minors to participate in open court meetings. In any event, the court's consent to the participation of a minor would be necessary. The draft implements the petition submitted to the Senate in March 2016. The petitioner pointed out that, in the current legal state, the President's consent to the presence of minors in the courtroom only in criminal proceedings, whereas the courts deal with a number of matters relevant to the community and observe the conduct of public hearings would have educational advantages and would prepare minors for active participation in public life. In the opinion of the draftsman, such a solution ensures the freedom of decision of the chairman of the formation of the court, who will take an individual decision, taking into account inter alia. the smooth course of the meeting or the possible demoralisation of the minor by the content presented during the meeting. It is also intended to allow parents and children with whom there is no other care to enter the courtroom when the parent takes part in the hearing, e.g. as a party or as a witness. According to the draft, the authorisation will be granted in the form of an order from which it will have recourse to the court of judgment. The provisions on the authorisation of a minor to participate in a public hearing issued outside the hearing will be open to challenge, but the chairman may revoke or amend them if the circumstances of the case change. Giving minors the right to be present, in their capacity as public, in open civil proceedings before a general court and in administrative matters decided by an administrative court, shall be fully supported by the Ombudsman of the rights of the child. On the other hand, the Supreme Court, in its opinion on the project, considers that children's access to the court should be restricted for their benefit. In addition, the presence of children in the courtroom undermines the seriousness of the court and may affect the quality of the explanations or testimony of the parent. According to the Supreme Court, a minor should have access to the courtroom after a certain age (the opinion indicates an example of the age of 16 years). High Izbo! In our view, allowing minors to attend court meetings will strengthen the subjectivity of children, enabling them to participate actively in public life, to gain knowledge and experience in civil and administrative matters. It will also help to shape respect for the law and strengthen the sense of justice in children. The proposed change is the implementation of the right of the child to information, which is essential to meet the educational needs, the school obligation or the preparation of the child for an active life in civil society. With regard to the arguments of the Supreme Court, paying attention to the protection of the child's welfare, we believe that the child's well-being will be sufficiently protected by the opinion of the President, who will each issue a decision on the admission of minors to the courtroom. High Izbo, on behalf of the PSL Members' Club, I request that the project be directed to further work on the committee.

Here is a quote: In exceptional circumstances, it is even possible to provide for certain derogations from the lex retro non agit rules if this is justified by the need to implement another constitutional principle, and at the same time it is not possible to implement this principle without retroactive action of the law. The admissibility of a derogation from these constitutional rules depends on the importance of the constitutional values which the law under examination is intended to protect. This is the judgment of 7 February 2001. Ladies and gentlemen, why am I talking about this? The fact that Polish law did not work at the right time is not the fault of the citizens. The fact that Polish courts do not work and this law is not enforced is not the fault of citizens. The fact that there was no political will in the Polish Parliament to solve this problem is not the fault of the citizens. The fact that Directive 93/13 has not been consistently guarded as a sanctity in consumer rights is not the fault of citizens. This is a failure of the Polish state. As Members, we have an opportunity to rehabilitate. We are talking about a kind of rehabilitation, restoring what has been corrupted by us here in this House. And such prohibited provisions shall be deemed to be according to the draft order, first, specifying in the foreign currency a loan or loan which has been paid in Polish currency, and secondly, indexing or value-setting foreign currency a loan or loan specified and paid in Polish currency. It shall be possible for the court to consider the contractual order inadmissible only in the case of a cumulative fulfilment of the two conditions laid down in Article. 385 Civil Code, i.e. the development of consumer rights and obligations in a manner contrary to good practices and gross infringement of his interests. Therefore, the extension of the catalogue by these two provisions proposed in this proposal is, in our view, fully justified. In the case of loans, e.g. so-called. Franc loans, the obligations of the bank's customers were accepted in a manner contrary to good practices, as well as a gross violation of the interests of those borrowers. Referring to the proposal in question to European Union law, it should be referred to Council Directive 93/13/EEC on unfair terms in consumer contracts. Art. 3 of this directive was implemented in the Polish law in precisely Article. 385 Civil Code. According to the designers, the amendments proposed in the current draft are not included in the catalogue of provisions contained in the Directive, but this is not an obstacle to the extension of this catalogue in Polish law, as Member States may adopt stricter legislation in order to ensure a higher degree of consumer protection. In fact, the adoption of the proposed law will only result in the obligation for the Council of Ministers to provide information on this regulation to the European Union. There is still a response to the article. 2 of the project, which states that the provisions introduced also apply to contracts concluded before the date of entry into force of the act and therefore the law has been applied retroactively. This principle of non-retroactivity, according to the Constitutional Court, is the basis of the legal order, but in exceptional circumstances this is acceptable. For this project, the protection of consumer rights is a constitutional value as well as one of the fundamental principles of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union.
