ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u438	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The subject of my speech is the draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and some other laws. Why would you even think about changing those rules? Well, I am a driver who travels several thousand kilometers a month, just as most of your fellow Members probably do. It is a natural activity, of which we do not think about it on a daily basis. However, radically our approach changes at a time when an unexpected situation occurs, such as a collision, in which there is no certainty as to who is the perpetrator of the event. With similar problems and with many other problems, drivers from all over Poland applied to the Law Association on the Road, which is the main author of the proposed law, headed by the President of Artur Mezglewski. This project provides a number of traffic-related issues. At present, objective truth does not apply in court. Of course, this does not apply to all judges, but this is how the judiciary behaves in its mass. In many ways, nothing has changed since the commune. Of course, the rules have changed, but practice has not changed. What does that mean? This means that a commanding officer decides who to refer to for punishment and who not to direct him against. It's a bullshit court, and 90% of the cases are court-martialed by an officer. Of course, there are plenty of drivers of croissants who don't plead guilty to the principle, but there are also a whole bunch of wrongly wronged people. The Act consists of two segments: administrative and criminal. The administrative segment, more specifically, concerns traffic control. At present, there is no definition of control, conditions, grounds, and above all, no controlled rights. We propose to civilise these issues without taking away any of the powers of the police. The penalty segment concerns the fact that we introduce the principle of material evidence in infringements cases without effect, i.e. That the testimony of the officer who was in charge couldn't be evidence in the case. It can be a recorded measurement or even a simple video recording. The introduction of such a rule will relieve the courts. Most cases disappear from the courts, they don't get to them at all. If the police don't have proof, they won't file a case in court, and on the other hand, the citizen won't go to court when there's obvious evidence of his guilt. Not only do we relieve the courts, but also the police. It takes a month to prepare an application for punishment, and after our bill it will take 15 minutes. We're gonna re-bureaucratize the police. At the moment, almost all road traffic offences are referred to a pre-emptive procedure. This is a waste of time, because if the citizen has not accepted the mandate, he will also disagree with the court order, which means three months of loss. The Act also provides a simple enforcement of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of October 2016. In colloquially speaking, we remove the administrative mode of driving licenses for driving more than 50 km/h, and we transfer them to criminal law. The driving ban for up to 3 months will be ruled by a court, not by an old lady, not immediately, but in the event of a court determination. We are introducing a new criminal measure in the form of stopping a driver's license, but it will be decided by the court. The guilty will not escape punishment, but he will have the opportunity to fight for his rights in a fair trial. We relieve the police of obligations they shouldn't be burdened with. Instead of wandering the courts, the police will eventually have time to carry out their main tasks. In addition, the Law of 20 June 1997 on Road Traffic makes the following amendments. In the absence of documents presented by the manager, the competent authority shall verify the powers of the manager in the electronic system, possibly in the nearest police unit. The driver of a vehicle who does not show evidence of entitlement to drive shall comply with the instructions of the competent authority to determine the above. allowances, in particular expect the results of the verification, regardless of the duration of the verification operation, and in the case of e.g. the system failure is required to go to the nearest police unit in order to determine the above. allowances. We are introducing a temporary withdrawal of the vehicle - a change that many Poles are waiting for. It shall be carried out at the request of the owner of the vehicle or the body entrusted with the vehicle in accordance with Article. Article 75 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5, the governor competent due to the location of the last registration of the vehicle, issuing a decision on the temporary withdrawal of the vehicle. The vehicle may be temporarily withdrawn from service for the period indicated in the application by the owner of the vehicle or the operator entrusted with the vehicle and this period may be extended or shortened by a decision of the competent authority. Of course, the owner of the vehicle or the entity entrusted with the vehicle is obliged to ensure that the vehicle is withdrawn from traffic outside the public road, residential area and traffic zone. The Minister responsible for Transport shall determine, by means of a regulation, the mode and conditions of temporary withdrawal of the vehicle, the amount of the charges depending on the period of withdrawal from traffic not higher than PLN 150 for the decision to temporarily withdraw the vehicle in this respect, having regard to the need to prevent the use of vehicles temporarily withdrawn from traffic and the costs incurred by public administrations. In addition, the concept of routine control will disappear after the law has been implemented. The grounds for stopping a road inspection shall be the reasonable suspicion that a traffic offence has been committed and the reasonable suspicion that participation in the traffic of a particular driver or vehicle poses a risk to other traffic participants. We end with a situation where a good driver moving in a fully functioning car according to traffic regulations from the vending machine becomes a potential suspect for police patrol. The Act clearly sets out the duties and rights of the officer and the person controlled. The course of traffic control shall be recorded using the image recording device and sound. To the article. 130 is added to the paragraph. 1b as follows: Only those formal infringements which have been registered by the recording device, the measuring and checking instrument or other equipment recording the image and sound shall be entered in the records. Here we enter into the main assumption of the law - personal cameras for police officers. Recorded footage is evidence. What a police officer sees is also registered by the device. This allows us radically to avoid any irregularities during the inspection. It disappears almost 80% cases of discourse or improper conduct, both by the police officer carrying out the inspection and by the manager. We also propose that the Act of 5 January 2011 on driving vehicles should introduce the following amendment. To the article. Paragraph 98 of the Act is added. 5a as follows: A driver who has not received any penalty points in a given calendar year shall receive a bonus in the form of 5 positive points as from 1 January of the following year. This idea was supposed to be a kind of distinction for drivers who drive very well and do not get any tickets. This is the record that in turn aroused the most controversy, so we put it under discussion, and if it is the will of the majority, we will not insist on leaving it for strength. The Act of 24 August 2001 introduces the Code of Conduct for Offences, inter alia. Amendment to Article 7, which reads: The police and other authorities in criminal proceedings shall carry out the orders of the court and shall carry out investigations within the limits set out in the Act. In relation to the tasks performed by police officers who act as public prosecutor, the provisions on service liability shall not apply. A very important article is also inserted. The following paragraph 3 shall be added: In court proceedings, participation in proceedings may be declared by a social organisation. That hasn't happened yet. Art. 54 § 7 shall be replaced by the following: "The hearing of the person referred to in paragraph 6 shall be refused if the person concerned signs a declaration that he does not wish to be heard. That person shall be instructed to inform the investigating authority of any change in his/her place of residence or stay lasting more than 7 days. The person may send explanations to the competent authority within 7 days of the withdrawal from the hearing, as to which he should be instructed. The withdrawal from the hearing and the instruction shall be documented by an official note. Then we amend the rule in Article. § 97 § 2: The perpetrator of the offence may refuse to accept a criminal mandate. It may also request that its decision on the adoption of a criminal mandate be postponed within 7 days. You'll ask why. So that the citizen may have time to consider a possible consultation with the legal counsel. Often confused drivers, when they hear from an officer the word "mandat" or to the court, prefer to accept a mandate for peace of mind. In conclusion, as I mentioned at the beginning, this is a project written by citizens for citizens. We are sure that many provisions of the Act will meet with your support, but there will certainly be one that needs to be discussed. That's what we're counting on. I encourage all of us to work together on this project in committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Article 2 and Article 2 Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution and should be amended. The proposals for simplification of the mandate procedure also draw attention. The institution of criminal points is an important tool to promote road safety. Applicants are mistakenly assumed that it is a problem to direct more drivers, too many drivers, to repeat exams. However, the problem is road safety and the need for participants to comply with the rules. So the proposals that can break this system are de facto contrary to the social interest. Further controversial changes are the possibility of temporarily suspending registration of a vehicle resulting in suspension of compulsory insurance. The applicants did not provide enough convincing arguments to argue that such a legal situation would lead to the fragmentation of the insurance system, which is also confirmed by the opinion of the Polish Insurance Association. A number of amendments concern the provisions of the Code in cases of misconduct. The proposal to repeal the Article deserves to be rejected. 2 § 1a, which provides that judgment in ordinary proceedings is to take place where there is no basis for the case to be heard in an accelerated or prescriptive procedure. Ratio legis of this provision is the possibility of speeding up and simplifying criminal proceedings by restricting the recognition of cases in ordinary proceedings. Undoubtedly, the repeal of this provision will have a negative impact on the efficiency of ruling in simple matters. Another change which raises a number of doubts both in the area of legal doctrine and practical aspects is the possibility for the accused to resign from the limitation. Similar reservations concern the use or introduction of the concept: formal misconduct. In fact, these are reservations that disqualify these proposals, which is also reflected and confirmed by the Supreme Court's opinion. Our concerns are also raised by proposals for amendments to the Code of Criminal Procedure. Both the absolute ban on hearing an officer in a mandate procedure and the repeal of the obligation to indicate in the request for justification the scope of the statement of reasons are proposals that will de facto affect the rights of individual stakeholders, but also the effectiveness of the investigation into the truth. They are therefore not accepted. In conclusion, the bill contains a number of proposals which do not allow a general positive assessment of this submission, but can indeed be a possible reason for discussing the target solutions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u480	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of Messengers and Members of Modernity, I have the honour to present to you a draft law amending the Environmental Law and the Road Traffic Law Act. Before I move on to a detailed presentation of the changes we have proposed, let me briefly refer to the problem of poor air quality that we have to deal with almost every day. The smog problem is not a theoretical problem. The problem of smog, which we have been able to find out in the last few weeks, concerns all of us. According to the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber and the European Environment Agency, Poland has for years had the most polluted air throughout the European Union. Smog kills. It's the cause of 48,000. Deaths per year. Out of the 50 most polluted European cities, 33 are Polish cities, mainly located in the south of our country. This is on the one hand a cause for shame, but for us politicians, this should be the last moment to adopt radical solutions to improve air quality. I must add with sadness that the last year in this respect is completely lost. The government majority has not prepared any solutions to improve this state, and worse, it has taken decisions which have the effect of halting the financing of programmes aimed at inter alia. replacement of boilers or thermomodernisation of buildings. A modern antismog decalogue has been prepared. Among the proposed solutions we propose, among others. the programme for the expansion of district heating networks, the co-financing of the conversion of boilers to modern, class V standards, the programme for the support for the exchange of low-carbon collective transport rolling stock, the promotion of collective transport measures, subsidies for low-income households using clean fuels, the regulation setting standards for boilers and fuel quality - but this cannot be a sham action, as is the case with the draft regulations - civic air monitoring and an effective control system, education programmes raising citizens' awareness or, finally, legal changes enabling the establishment of temporary or permanent zones or permanent emissions. The fight against smog should be a priority of the government today, implemented in cooperation with local governments. For many cities today, this is the most important challenge they face. These cities expect legal solutions and real support programmes for municipal low emission reduction programmes. One of the largest sources of air pollution is communication pollution, including emissions from the movement of vehicles driven by internal combustion engines. In the current state of law, local authorities cannot take real action to significantly reduce communication pollution. Like solutions adopted in many European cities, the Modern Posel Club, taking into account the calls of non-governmental and local communities, proposes a bill amending the Environmental Law and the Road Traffic Law which allows the city council to establish a population of more than 200 000. a restricted or prohibited zone for emissions from the movement of vehicles with internal combustion engines by introducing restrictions or prohibitions on entry into the zone for vehicles not complying with the established Euro emission standards. At present, there are no regulations in Poland that would introduce transport restrictions due to poor air quality or allow competent authorities to introduce such restrictions. On the other hand, emissions from modes of transport have a major impact on air quality, especially in cities with a large number of inhabitants. The transport sector is a source of air pollutants such as nitrogen oxides, carbon monoxide, aromatic hydrocarbons, heavy metals and PM10 and PM2.5 dust. Communication pollution is today the second, after emissions from domestic ovens, source of air pollution and at national level, their share is already 9%. - this is the data recorded in the national air protection programme by 2020. In large cities this share is much larger. For example, in Cracow it exceeds 20%, and in Warsaw communication pollution in peak periods has an 80% share and they themselves exceed the permitted standards - 65 micrograms per cubic meter. Added to the Environmental Law Art. 96b provides the possibility for the city/city council to establish more than 200 000 inhabitants. the restricted or banned exhaust emissions of vehicles driven by internal combustion engines. The municipality's municipal council's resolution under this provision will have to, it is mandatory, contain the elements specified therein specifying the territorial scope of the zone, its restrictions, prohibitions and orders laid down in relation to the emission requirements for exhaust pollutants. In determining the area covered by the zone, account should be taken of the need to ensure transit traffic. The resolution will also allow exemptions from its prohibitions or orders. I asked for an opinion on the changes we proposed to the presidents of 16 cities, those cities to which this law may have its reference, to which it may affect. And so, for example, I will quote three opinions which, I think, are very important, and I immediately point out that most of the presidents have responded to the request for an opinion and that these are only positive opinions. For example, the Deputy President of Warsaw writes: The introduction of the proposed changes will also be the implementation of some of the postulates presented in the position taken on 12 January 2016 by the largest Polish cities concentrated in the Union of Polish Metropolises, i.e. the transfer of competences to local governments regarding the possibility of establishing in city centres restricted communication emission zones and the dependence of the authorisation to enter these vehicle zones on the fulfilment of relevant emission standards. Systemic solutions adopted at national legislation level will help to increase the effectiveness of action to reduce emissions and improve air quality. The President of Krakow informs that the initiative presented is fully justified, that he already spoke to the Minister of the Environment in 2011, asking for the necessary changes to the air quality issues to be included in the legislative initiatives, and that this law will be another tool for an effective fight for clean air. Similarly, President Częstochowa's opinion is positive, which declares that if the changes in question enter into force, they will seek to ensure that the Częstochowa City Council establishes a permanent or periodic zone of restricted or prohibited traffic of vehicles driven by internal combustion engines, which will be properly marked. I would like to declare that the proposals for changes made by the presidents of cities will mostly try to introduce this law into it. I would also like to say that this project complies with European Union legislation, with Directive 2008/50/EC. Moreover, the Sejm Analysis Office stresses that Poland's correct implementation of this directive has been raised by the European Commission on several occasions. Last year, the Commission called on Poland to take action on air pollution, to comply with European Union rules obliging citizens to reduce their exposure to small PM10 particles. It is also worth remembering that on 15 June Ub. Once again, the Commission brought an action against Poland before the Court of Justice of the European Union. The introduction of these zones should contribute to reducing transport emissions and thus improve urban air quality. The proposed amendment itself will not entail the need for public expenditure. However, labelling costs will have to be incurred if the City Council decides to take advantage of the powers conferred on it by the law and establishes a zone with limited transport emissions. The costs incurred for the supply and distribution of vehicle labelling labels will only be charged temporarily to the budget of the local government unit, since the entity to whom they will be issued will ultimately be obliged to cover them according to the assumptions adopted. Please, Members, so that we can work on this bill in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Aye, aye, Marshal.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u481	The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. Here we go. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Rafał Weber on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u482	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to give our opinion on printing no. 1199, i.e. on amending the provisions of the Environmental Law and the Law on Road Traffic. I will point out some very serious shortcomings in this law, which in our opinion discredits it. Firstly, it should be noted that the justification for the project does not state whether local authorities have declared the validity and purpose of introducing restricted or prohibited vehicle traffic zones. Legal solutions in this area should clearly meet the rationale of purpose, which will ensure that the law developed is applied in practice. 16 local authorities are included in this project, while only 3 opinions were quoted by the applicant Member. We do not know the position of other local governments. Another deficiency is related to the proposal to add an article. 96b in the Environmental Law Act, allowing the introduction of a resolution which is an act of local law of a fixed or periodic zone limited or prohibited movement of vehicles driven by internal combustion engines. It should be noted that the provision currently proposed confers exclusive competence on the City Council to establish the abovementioned zones, without having to take into account the requirements of road managers arising from the need to ensure the coherence of the road network. Both in the draft version of the article. 96b. Point 2 (1) and the explanatory memorandum of the draft law indicate that the designated area covered by the zone should take account of the need to ensure transit traffic, but in practice, it will be possible, for example, to designate a restricted or prohibited zone for vehicles driven by internal combustion engines. the closure for residents of a national road performing a transit function through the city, irrespective of the possibility to determine its alternative course. Another point is that it should also be pointed out that the legislative delegation proposed in the draft seems insufficient to issue a regulation. Issues covered by the proposed delegation need to be regulated by law. This includes: the identification of the operators issuing the marking confirming the emission class, the indication that the revenue from the labelling fees and the imposition of a penalty for the failure to mark the vehicle will constitute the revenue of the State budget. It should be stressed that the implementing rules cannot generate financial consequences beyond those arising from the law. It is not clear from the draft law whether the marking would be subject to all vehicles in the country. The completely overlooked issue is how to deal with cars registered in other countries. It is not clear whether the labelling would be common to all cities, or whether it could be differentiated. It also appears essential that the justification for the project does not fully indicate the social impact of the proposed regulation, i.e. the impact on citizens - global and concrete - and the economic impact of banning certain vehicles. There is also no indication whether the introduction of these zones in other countries has affected the level of air pollution. In view of the above, the number of deficiencies and their seriousness, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice makes an application for rejection in full in the first reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Environmental Law and the Law on Road Traffic contained in print No 1199. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The ban applies to both passenger cars and commercial vehicles and motorcycles. From 1 January 2008, only vehicles meeting certain exhaust requirements - red, yellow and green - may move in the designated part of the city. From 1 January 2010, only Euro 4 cars with a green label are allowed into the strict centre. For lack of a badge or entry by an unauthorized car, a fine of EUR 40 is at risk. Polish lands located in the 19th century under partitions suffered from a shortage of institutions in which Polish science would develop. Franciszek Ksawery Malinowski must be judged extremely highly in scientific but also patriotic terms. This effort of the company was organized in honor of outstanding Poles, Adam Mickiewicz and Fryderyk Chopin. On the first occasion of the century his birthday was dedicated to a special meeting with a public reading about his work, and for the same reason the company erected a board with a bust of the composer on its building. Under the conditions of universal terror of the aggressor seeking to eliminate traces of Polishness from public life, these actions must be considered exceptional, and there were many more. Bearing in mind the important role of the society during the partitions, it is not possible to overlook its great role and fruitful activity during the period of independence of the Polish state in 1918 and 1939. On this occasion we raise the initiative to create the University of Poznań, but since 1923 the company has become a very scientific institution, which resulted in the publication of such outstanding works as the letters of Zygmunt Krasiński to the Potocka Dolphin or Tadeusz Grabowski's history of Polish literature. The company reactivated in 1945 despite the unfavorable circumstances of functioning under communist conditions continued to work for the benefit of the Polish people. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u530	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the point: the report of the committee on the parliamentary draft resolution on the celebration of the 160th anniversary of the establishment of the Poznań Society of Friends of Sciences, 1307 and 1311 prints. The Society of Friends of the Sciences of Poznań - the oldest name of this association - first gathered on 13 February 1857. The founding members were 42, among them the main initiators: Dr. Kazimierz Szulc, Fr. Franciszek Ksawery Malinowski, Titus Działyński. The uprising of PTPN was a Polish response to the policy of the Prussian state, which did not allow the development of national culture, science, where there was no possibility of establishing and operating a Polish university. At the beginning of its activities, the association was primarily humanistic and artistic. But at a rapid pace, he began expanding his scientific fields through the departments where regular scientific work was carried out. The Faculty of Natural Sciences conducted western Poland physiography studies, organized a chemical laboratory, and even the Faculty of Medicine was established. It was thanks to the scientific infrastructure created by PTPN and its initiative that it was possible to establish a life at the beginning of independence, already in 1919, a Polish university in Poznań. During World War II, the library collection was largely dispersed and partially destroyed, the archive was destroyed, the building was devastated. After the war, in 1945, the PTPN Historical Commission resumed its work. Physiographic research has been resumed slowly, publishing, popularizing and library activities have been initiated. Unfortunately, the people's authorities, which did not favour PTPN, caused not only the loss of some of the company's assets, but most importantly, the fall of the social spirit after 1981 and the weakening of the energy of its members. The contribution of the Poznań Society of Friends of Sciences to the development of not only the Greater Poland, but also the national academic and cultural thought should be without question appreciated. The world's first monument to Adam Mickiewicz, the creation of the Piast Allergy, or later the University of Piast. Adam Mickiewicz, or the creation of a base for the future National Museum in Poznań, is only a few of the long list of merits of the Poznań Society of Friends of Sciences for Polish national culture. The Modern Posel Club of course supports this and will vote in favour of the motion for a resolution on the celebration of the 160th anniversary of the establishment of the Poznań Society of Friends of Sciences.

He was born in Silesia, Rybnik, in March 1921 in a large family of Józef Blaclinicki and Maria Miller. He was a student of the Tarno Góra middle school and a Boy Scout. In 1938, he graduated and went to the army. In 1939 he took part in the September campaign. After the surrender, he began his conspiracy. Wanted and captured, is transported to Auschwitz. He survives in the camp for 14 months, nine of which are in the penal company and a month in the bunker. Taken from there to Zabrze and Katowice, he is sentenced to death by beheading. After more than four months, he is pardoned. The sentence was changed to 10 years in prison after the end of the war. In Katowice, Fr. Balnicki, a convict, converts and dedicates his life to the service of Christ. It mentions the gift of faith, the gift of the vision of the living Church including the program of life and action, the gift of the Second Vatican Council and its vision of the Church, communio - this action is closed in the charism of the Movement of Light-Life. The third gift is the gift of the Immaculate Community, the Mother of the Church. And the fourth gift is the gift to make ourselves a complete gift. Father Franciszek Blaclinicki is in camps and prisons from 1942 to 1945. In 1945 he returns to the country and joins the Silesian Seminary in Krakow. In 1950 he became a priest. Maximilian Kolby. On his return from Immaculate Poland, he works in the Katowice curia and the Sunday Guest, he appoints the Crusade of Abstinence - a movement of religious and moral renewal. Francis takes up studies at KUL and writes a bachelor's degree in survival and education of children's closed retreats. Returning to his second gift, which he considers to be the most important, he defends his PhD. He's not approved by the Ministry of Education. In protest, Fr. Balnicki resigns from work at KUL and continues to work on the concept of personal theology and post-storial liturgy. The necessary for the development of tourism are the creation of new transport routes, the construction of parking lots, the creation of a bike route around the lake, modern recreational, tourist and sports infrastructure. These works must go hand in hand with the cleaning and deepening of the lake floors, flood protection of the lake, all streams and rivers that are its tributaries, the management of lakes and rivers, and the modernisation of the water supply and sewage and rainwater networks. The Lake Żywieckie has every opportunity to become a model of Swiss lakes with a real pearl. It could successfully attract tourists not only from Poland, but also foreign tourists - from Slovakia, the Czech Republic, and be a stimulating factor for Żywiecczyn. In order to do this, it is necessary to cooperate in obtaining funds between the municipalities and the Żywiecki district and the management of water management, the authorities of the Śląskie Voivodeship, the Minister of Development and the Minister of Environment, which I ask from this point of view and cordially appeal for. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u544	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! February 23rd this year We celebrate the 95th anniversary of the birthday of Scouts and AK soldiers Stanisław Magura and his twin brother John. On this occasion, I would like to say a few words about the life of these so-called ones. The Grey Private twins who died fighting the occupier. Stanisław and John were born on February 23, 1922 in the Potok. They graduated from the general school in Krosno and then attended the State Secondary School and the General Secondary School. Nicolaus Copernicus in Krosno, where in 1939 they graduated from the first high school class. And although they had different interests - Jan was interested in building airplanes and architecture, Stanisław was considering studying at the Academy of Fine Arts - they were also passionate about working together in ZHP. In the mid-30s, they participated in a scouting rally in Spala. In 1938 they completed the Podharcmaster's course in Folusz. In the same year Stanisław was elected President of the Polish Tourist and Nationalistic Society. Ignacy Łukasiewicz in Krosno. In August 1939, the brothers obtained category B of the glider pilot. They were both very athletic. During the first days of World War II, they served in the military upbuilding unit. Before the Germans entered Krosno, they and their father left for Lviv intending to join the Polish Army. However, when the Soviets entered, they decided to return to Krosn, crossing the illegally filled German and Soviet border posts. There were other fugitives with them then, among them. An official of the city council of Zenon Saturday - a later participant in the shares of the guest house - with his wife and daughters. In December 1939 the brothers took an active part in the action of the Polish Red Cross, which helped Poles displaced from the western lands of Poland. Later, they were both involved in organizing aid in crossing the border for refugees going to the army in France. They began their service in conspiracy at the Secret Military Organization - Jan under pseudo. A hummingbird, then a Pirate, Stanisław pod pseudod. Peacock, then splinter. Then they both got involved with the Grey Troops, taking part in many sabotage-diversity actions. In 1942. Stanisław went to work where he had access to classified documents. He took every opportunity to obtain valuable information. On the night of 5th and 6th August 1943, he participated in a prison robbery in Jasła - an action under the code name "Pension" - which was then assessed as the greatest feat of the resistance movement in southern Poland. For his heroic attitude in this action he was awarded the Cross of Valor. On January 3, 1944, the Gestapo officers arrested the engineer. Louis and Florentine of Madeira with the sons of Ludwik and Zdzisław. The next day they captured the brothers John and Stanisław Magur and Kazimierz Pietruszka. Arab. The arrested were accused of collaborating with Zenon Sobota and contributing to the division of the Jasiel prison. During a cruel investigation, despite the cruel torture of the beasts, the Magur brothers did not break or reveal any secrets. Zenon Saturday tried to rescue the prisoners, unfortunately unsuccessfully. They were deported from Jasła and then registered in the Kraków prison at ul. Montelupi. The mother received from her sons a demand for packages. Sadly, at the beginning of February, there was no trace of the first, and at the end of February, there was no trace of the second brother. John was probably killed in a collective execution of prisoners in the woods near Grębowa. There's no information on how Stanisław died. Their resting places are also unknown. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u545	Declaration on the Restoration of the Energy Distribution and Connection Point in Rypin. The time of government of the PO-PSL coalition is not only difficult years for Polish energy, it is also multiplying into the power of difficulties for citizens. Under the motto of modernity and cost cuts, jobs were reduced and power stations were removed, customer service offices were closed, and all help and information were dismissed, giving the already exemplified formula about calling the hotline. How much trouble these decisions have caused for ordinary electricity consumers, long to calculate. The reduction in employment resulted in infrastructure failure, the maintenance of transmission networks was not properly maintained, which will not remain without the consequences of failure. In my parliamentary work, I have once again been able to take part in real action to change this negative trend in energy. A week ago, 17 February, the Energy Distribution and Connection Service Centre in Rypin was restored. During the meeting with local government officials and representatives of ENERGYA, I had the honour to speak on behalf of the Minister of Energy Krzysztof Tchórzewski and read a letter in which the Minister congratulated the Mayor Paweł Grzybowski on his efforts to ensure that the inhabitants of Rypin and the Rypiński district could explain the electrical issues on the spot. Until recently, with each glitch, they had to go to a distance of about 25 km from Brodnica, resulting in additional costs and delays in repairs. The response time will now be significantly reduced, and in addition about 30 employees will take action on the spot without commuting to work in Brodnica. I would like to point out that the facility in Rypin is one of three reactivated regional connection points of ENERGY Operator, because the activity resumed the facilities in Starogard Gdański and Radziejów. We are therefore facing a positive trend in looking at such an important and indispensable service as the provision of energy, finally from the level of citizens' needs. It may seem that the PO-PSL coalition has acted in the opposite direction, as many of the energy sector's employees have defined. as a preparation of a situation in which ENERGYA could be privatised. This negative attitude is due to thanks to the Minister of Energy, Mr Krzysztof Tchórzewski, and at the local level to Mayor Pawł Grzybowski, whom I congratulate on his determination and effect, for the benefit of the inhabitants of Rypin. Thanks to such actions, as noted in the letter addressed to the mayor, Minister, as the only local government in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship and one of only five cities in Poland Rypin won the title of "Energy-friendly" in October last year in a competition organized by the Polish Society for Transmission and Distribution of Electricity. Probably the restoration of the Energy Distribution and Connection Point in Rypin is only the beginning of good decisions, because they stem from the new strategy for the development of the ENERGY Group, whose activities are now to focus on the customer and development of modern energy infrastructure, i.e. what was forgotten for the government of Donald Tusk and Ewa Kopacz.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-23-sejm-36-2.u547	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On February 20th, we celebrated the anniversary of a soldier cursed Anatol Radzivonik ps. Olech, lieutenant of the Polish Army, since 1943 in the National Army, one of the commanders of troops of the anti-communist underground and independence, the last commander of organized structures of the Polish underground of independence on New Zealand. We don't really know when Olech joined the conspiracy. We know for sure that in 1943 he was in charge of one of several conspiracies of the Rat circuit of the AK. He was promoted to lieutenant for his involvement. At the beginning of 1944, he switched conspiracy activities to guerrilla. He took command of the platoon in Second Company 7th Battalion 77. Infantry regiment AK under the command of the famous porch. Jan Piwnik, Ponure. In early 1944 he took command of the platoon in 7th Battalion 77. AK infantry regiment. He fought against Germany, among others. in Mayów Mały and Wielki and near Kowczyk. Together with his unit, he participated in many combat operations against Germany, e.g. in the victory of the liquidation of the German garrison in Jewish on 16 June 1944. At the beginning of July, his battalion set off for Vilnius to take part in an operation under the code name "Ostrzy Gate." The plan was to capture the Vilnius defended by the German garrison before the Soviets entered it. Unfortunately, the operation was accelerated and his squad didn't make it in time. It saved him from being disarmed by the Russians. The German occupation was converted into Soviet, but Olech did not intend to lay down his weapons. Together with his people, he decided to continue fighting on the Ends, confirming the Polishness of these lands. He was waiting for a peace conference, which he assumed would solve the issue of the Kresów in favour of Poland. Unfortunately, the Western Allies already agreed on the issue of the eastern border of Poland at a conference in Tehran in 1943. They did not inform their most faithful ally, namely Poland. In 1945, Olech was the commander of the Ratchczyn and Lida circuits. He also commanded a strong guerrilla branch (in 1945 he was subject to about 800 people). Once it was known that the eastern border of Poland would be based on Curzon's line, its troops began to gradually crumble. The radzivonik gave free rein to his commanding officer. Some of them decided to go to Poland and fight there, many resigned, many died in the NKVD manhunts. Massive attacks on guerrillas, which were, of course, divided into several smaller divisions, began at the beginning of 1949. Many soldiers died, among others. d.o.b. Witold Malen, Gypsy, who was the deputy chief. The noose around Radzivonik's neck was tightening more and more. On 12 May, together with several companions, he was surrounded by several rings of NKVD troops near Raczkowszczyzna. Only three survivors. He died on 12 May 1949 at the Niewish River while trying to break through the ring of another Soviet manhunt. Together with the death of Olecha, there was an end to organized resistance in Grodnoszczyzna. However, many guerrillas fought until the mid-50s. On the back of one of the pictures depicting the guerrillas of Radzivonik there is a inscription: Nothing for itself, everything for the Homeland. It was the motto of the line-up troops that the commander taught to his faithful soldiers. Let us remember this last commander of Grodnoszczyzna, especially during the upcoming National Day of Remembrance, Soldiers of the Cursed.
